### Totals

| Area | Pages | Rows | Record-only rows | Cells (state x mode x viewport) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| student | 4 | 12 | 0 | 342 |
| auth | 5 | 20 | 10 | 210 |
| fallback | 1 | 4 | 0 | 24 |
| admin | 24 | 48 | 12 | 1000 |
| **Pages and routes** | **34** | **84** | **22** | **1576** |
| Shared-region rows | 9 rows | n/a | n/a | 430 |
| Redirect rows | 5 rows | n/a | n/a | 60 observations |

Check: every page row's cells = states x 2 modes x viewports (3 for student, auth and fallback rows; 2 for admin rows); a shared-region row's cells also multiply by its roles. The 22 record-only rows are 10 redirects (the signed-in S, I and A rows of S-05 to S-07 and the signed-out row of S-09) and 12 defect records (the instructor rows of the admin-only pages). Total verification cells: **2006** (1576 on pages, 430 on shared regions), plus 60 redirect observations.

### Reading the states

- **Ids.** `loaded` is the default populated view (`loaded:<variant>` for a tab or a variant); `loading`, `empty`, `error`, `success` and `submitting` are what they say; `ov:<name>` is an overlay with its own content (dialog, panel, popover, banner, drawer, slash menu). Plain select menus and tooltips are component states (Phase 3) and are not rows.
- **`native:<name>`** is drawn by the browser (`confirm()`, `alert()`, `prompt()`). Stage 1 cannot restyle it, so the check is its wording and its trigger, not a contrast pass; the cell is kept so that no state of the spec goes unlisted.
- **`→ X`** is a hand-over: the state leaves for another page or region (a redirect after a successful submit, a panel opened elsewhere). It has no cells here; the destination's own rows verify what the user then sees.
- **What counts as a state.** A distinct view of the page that a States bullet or an overlay item of the spec names. Opening a row, an accordion or a select menu, hover and focus are interactions inside a view, not states of their own; Phase 3 specifies them with the components.
- **States per role.** A verified row lists the page's states for the role as the page is meant to work. Exclusions are real differences of the page for that role, not guard refusals: A-01 has no create dialog for I; S-02 shows comment pins and the summary banner only to an enrolled account. A verified row whose Today is B2 (I on A-16 and A-17) keeps all the page's states, and the Role outcomes cell says what the instructor gets today. A defect record lists none: the state it should show is a no-permission state, which no page entry specifies.
- **Mechanism pointers** (`m:G-n` in a spec) mark a bullet that a mechanism row of the Role outcomes covers; they add no state.
- `loaded` needs seeded data; `empty` needs an account or class with none; `error` and `loading` need a failed or stalled request; `success` and `ov:*` need the action performed. The page specs mark which of these the 2026-10-16 tour could not reach (`[S]`).
- Several error states are console-only today. Where the page then looks empty (S-01, A-03) the spec files the bullet under `empty`; where it spins for ever (SR-2) under `class-loading`; so the cell checks what a user would see.
- Role-dependent differences worth a separate pass are the top bar (SR-1: the view-switch icon for I and A), the chat panel (SR-6e: the Experiment row for I and A), the admin sidebar and class sub-menu (SR-3: the entries by role), A-01 (title, search placeholder and create control differ for I), A-12 (the classless route redirects I), A-17 (two tabs for admins) and S-02 (comment pins and summary banner only for an enrolled account).

---

## Defect register

Every statement in the 2026-10-16 tour that names a conflict with the brief or a defect is listed once, classified against the **Stage 1 boundary** (brief, *Stage 1 boundary*: in = colour, typography, measure and spacing inside existing containers, radius, elevation, borders, focus states, the mascot, interface copy, outgoing email colours and copy; out = moving or resizing regions, adding or removing navigation, adding routes, changing flows). Statements that only describe the current look and conflict with nothing (the logout icon, the class-switcher chevron, the tab names) are not defects and are not listed. **B4 to B6 and SEC-1 are not tour statements**: they come from the source pass and the live checks of 2026-10-16, marked `[L]` (B4 and B5 from the user's decision on instructors in the admin area, B6 from a live check `[L]` of a deep link) and are listed with the outside-the-boundary findings; SEC-1, a security finding, has a table of its own.

**Method.** Heuristic = Nielsen's ten (1994); severity 0-4 = frequency x impact x persistence. One evaluator, working from the tour notes and the source, so ratings are provisional and should be re-rated against user data in Stage 2. Palette-only findings are rated 1 (cosmetic): the brand conflict is recorded in the brief, not rated as a usability harm. **"Attached to" is derived from the page entries**: a finding appears here only against the entries that carry it, and every in-boundary finding must have at least one. A defect of a shared region (SR-1, SR-5, SR-6) is also written on every page that hosts the region, so those pages are listed; a run of three or more is written "A-01 to A-24". A contrast finding cites its WCAG 2.2 success criterion beside the heuristic.

### Summary by severity

All 46 findings, highest severity first (5 at severity 3, 16 at severity 2, 25 at severity 1). **Fix** names a direction and the phase that owns it; it is not a design, and nothing in it is done in Phase 1. Findings marked "Stage 2" are outside the Stage 1 boundary. The detail, the tour location and the pages each finding is attached to are in the two tables after this one.

| Severity | Heuristic | Problem | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3 | 4 Consistency and standards (WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.3) | **TD-19** Sign-in aside: white wordmark on a light blend at 1.8 to 2.0:1 (computed), campus photo missing | Phase 2: a contrast rule for text on the aside (WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.3: 3:1 for the wordmark as large text, 4.5:1 for the tagline), with and without a photo; Phase 3: supply the photo or drop the dependency. Measure live before and after. **Release-blocking against done-when 2.** |
| 3 | 5 Error prevention | **B1** `/admin/*` is guarded by `ProtectedRoute` only: any signed-in user reaches the shell | Stage 2 or a separate bug fix: wrap the `/admin` layout in `AdminProtectedRoute` (`src/auth/AdminProtectedRoute.tsx`), which already guards four admin routes. This closes B1 for students only: the wrapper admits instructors (`src/auth/AdminProtectedRoute.tsx:40`), so instructors still reach the admin-only pages until B4 and D-1/D-8 are fixed. |
| 3 | 9 Help users recognize, diagnose and recover from errors | **B2** A permission failure signs the user out as "session expired" | Stage 2 or a separate bug fix: answer 403 for a permission failure (`../kiwi/src/auth/auth.guard.ts`), end the session only on 401 (`src/utils/middleware.ts:61-102`), and say "no access" on the page. |
| 3 | 9 Help users recognize, diagnose and recover from errors | **B4** An instructor on an admin-only page is signed out or bounced instead of seeing a no-permission state | Stage 2 or a separate bug fix: a role guard on the admin-only routes that renders a no-permission state, and the backend answering 403 rather than 401 for a role refusal (`../kiwi/src/auth/auth.guard.ts`), so that the session no longer ends (B2). |
| 3 | 9 Help users recognize, diagnose and recover from errors | **B5** The instructors of a class are refused on Enrolment and Class email | A separate bug fix: guard the roster, enrol and class-email routes with the assigned-instructor check of `InstructorClassGuard` instead of `ProfGuard` (`../kiwi/src/auth/auth.guard.ts:84-133`), and show the two sidebar entries to the instructors of the class. |
| 2 | 1 Visibility of system status | **TD-13** Model select: the dropdown arrow overlaps the model name | Phase 3: give the select right padding so the arrow clears the label; an upstream fix exists on `NYUSH-ML/kiwi-ui`, branch `fix/model-select-arrow-spacing`. |
| 2 | 8 Aesthetic and minimalist design | **TD-15** Three filled primary buttons side by side on the Scratchpad | Phase 3 and 7: one filled primary per region (the Feel rule); the other two outlined or text. |
| 2 | 4 Consistency and standards | **TD-21** Sidebar labels and page headings disagree; heading case varies | Phase 5: make every sidebar label equal its page title; one heading case. |
| 2 | 4 Consistency and standards | **TD-22** Switch colour has no rule: purple on one page, green on another | Phase 3: one rule for switch colour; status green stays on status surfaces (G1). |
| 2 | 4 Consistency and standards | **TD-24** Dates follow the browser locale inside an English UI | Phase 5 and 3: one shared date formatter with a fixed locale for the UI language (G7, G12). |
| 2 | 4 Consistency and standards | **TD-28** Class Analytics uses two accent hues in one view | Phase 6: one accent plus a neutral categorical scale for the intents (brief OQ2). |
| 2 | 1 Visibility of system status | **TD-29** The "Analyzing student questions…" note reads as a warning | Phase 3: a neutral in-progress tone and progress bar (G1). |
| 2 | 8 Aesthetic and minimalist design | **TD-34** Enroll Students toolbar buttons wrap onto two and three lines at about 960 px | Phase 3: the density map keeps toolbar buttons on one line from 960 to 1440 px (shorter labels, or an icon with the count). |

| B1 | `/admin/*` is not role-guarded in the frontend: the layout route is wrapped in `ProtectedRoute` only (`src/App.tsx:129-135`), and only A-04, A-05, A-07 and A-08 add `AdminProtectedRoute`. The intended model is that students cannot enter `/admin/*`. What a student gets today differs by page ([Students on `/admin/*`](#students-on-admin-b1)): 5 render (A-01, A-02, A-12, A-18, A-23), 2 raise an `alert()` (A-14, A-24), 5 redirect (A-03, A-04, A-05, A-07, A-08) and 12 end in B2. | Tour, Bugs found on the way | 5 Error prevention | 3 | Students on `/admin/*`, every `/admin/*` entry that lists "B1 (guard)" (see its Guard field) and SR-3 | Out of the Stage 1 boundary: a guard and routing change. The intended access model says students cannot enter `/admin/*`; today's outcome per page is recorded as a guard section, not as matrix rows, and the fix is tracked separately. Linked to B4 and D-1/D-8: wrapping the layout in `AdminProtectedRoute` closes B1 for students only, because that wrapper admits instructors (`src/auth/AdminProtectedRoute.tsx:40`). |
| B2 | "Not permitted" signs the user out as "session expired": `AdminGuard`, `ProfGuard`, `InstructorClassGuard` and `InstructorRoleGuard` answer 401, not 403, and the middleware clears the session after a second 401 (`src/utils/middleware.ts:61-102`). It ends 12 admin pages for a student ([Students on `/admin/*`](#students-on-admin-b1)) and 12 for an instructor ([Role outcomes](#role-outcomes)); the student class shell never signs anyone out, because its class lookups answer 200 for every role. | Tour, Bugs found on the way | 9 Help users recognize, diagnose and recover from errors | 3 | S-05 (the banner), SR-3, Students on `/admin/*` and every admin entry whose Role outcomes cell reads B2 | Misleading message for a permission failure; a flow and behaviour change, Stage 2. |
| B4 | An instructor who opens an admin-only page is signed out (B2) or bounced with a browser `alert()` instead of seeing a no-permission state: 10 of the 12 pages sign the instructor out after the first frame, A-03 raises an `alert()` and redirects, and on A-02 the form renders and its submit signs the instructor out; `AdminProtectedRoute` even admits instructors on four of them (D-1), and the chat agent's sitemap offers A-02 and A-03 to instructors. Appendix A makes these 12 pages admin-only: A-02 (New class) to A-11, and in a class A-15 and A-22. | Not in the tour (source pass and the user's decision of 2026-10-16) | 9 Help users recognize, diagnose and recover from errors | 3 | Instructors on admin-only pages (A-02 to A-11, A-15, A-22), SR-3, D-8 | Out of the Stage 1 boundary: a new state and a guard change, with no page entry. Linked to B1 (the missing role guard on the `/admin` layout), B2 (a 401 treated as an expired session) and D-1, D-7, D-8; by the one rule the 12 instructor rows are record-only defect rows. |

### Security finding (outside the Stage 1 scope)

Not a usability finding, so it carries a security severity and no Nielsen rating, and it is kept apart from the Source-found register so that it is triaged on its own. This file gives only a pointer.

| ID | Finding | Security severity | Scope | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SEC-1 | Authorization gap on class enrolment endpoints, found from source on 2026-10-16. | **High** | Outside Stage 1's visual scope. | Details are kept out of this public repository until the fix is merged; the fix is tracked in a separate private task. **Owner and triage date: needed now.** |

### How the tour's five take-aways map to the register

| Tour take-away (Admin side) | Register entries |
|---|---|
| 1. One accent per view | TD-40 (instances TD-22, TD-28, TD-35) |
| 2. Switches, chips and KPI icons follow one colour rule each | TD-22 (switches), TD-35 (chips), TD-27 (KPI icons) |
| 3. Compact density has to handle about 960-1280 px without wrapping buttons or narrow columns | TD-34, TD-39 (re-check at 1280) |
| 4. The launcher needs a resting position that never covers content | TD-18 (position, out) and TD-05 (size and elevation, in) |
| 5. Copy: sidebar label equals page title, one heading case, no all-caps buttons, one date format | TD-21, TD-33, TD-10, TD-26, TD-16, TD-24 |

### Heuristic coverage

All ten of Nielsen's heuristics were walked against the tour and the source. "Findings" lists the register entries (tour findings); "Source leads" lists `SF-` items in Appendix B that bear on the same heuristic.

| # | Heuristic | Findings | Source leads and assessment |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Visibility of system status | TD-13, TD-29 (2) | SF-04 and SF-33 (spinners that never clear), SF-52 (no Stop while streaming), SF-54 (no offline detection). |
| 2 | Match between system and the real world | TD-02, TD-12, TD-20, TD-36, TD-38 (5) | Voice and terminology; the copy itself is Phase 5. |
| 3 | User control and freedom | TD-30, B6 (2) | SF-41 (no real deny on the OAuth screen), SF-52 (no Stop). |
| 4 | Consistency and standards | TD-C1, TD-03, TD-05, TD-07, TD-08, TD-09, TD-10, TD-11, TD-14, TD-16, TD-19, TD-21, TD-22, TD-23, TD-24, TD-26, TD-27, TD-28, TD-33, TD-37, TD-17 (21) | Contrast, legibility and palette findings are filed here (see the reading guide). The contrast rows (TD-19, and SF-01 and SF-42 in Appendix B) are text contrast and cite WCAG 2.2 SC 1.4.3. |
| 5 | Error prevention | B1 (1) | SF-24 (role change, access removal and password reset with no confirmation or undo), SF-49 (PDF annotation edits and deletes with no confirmation), SF-50 (one trash icon, three verbs, a native `confirm()`). |
| 6 | Recognition rather than recall | TD-25 (1) | SF-18 and SF-34 (icon-only controls with no visible text and, in SF-18, no accessible name). |
| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency of use | none (0) | **No finding.** The tour did not exercise accelerators and Stage 1 changes no flows. What the source offers experienced users: Enter sends and Shift+Enter breaks the line, and slash commands, in the chat composer (SR-6, whose placeholder is their only cue); bulk selection with bulk approve or reject (A-10) and bulk email or remove (A-16); the last PDF and page restored per class (S-02), the editor width remembered (S-03) and the launcher's position remembered (SR-6). None of it needs a Stage 1 change. |
| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist design | TD-01, TD-04, TD-15, TD-31, TD-32, TD-34, TD-35, TD-39, TD-40, TD-06, TD-18 (11) | Decoration, filled-button count and wrapping; SF-56 to SF-62 add the filled-primary count on seven admin pages. |
| 9 | Help users recognize, diagnose and recover from errors | B2, B4, B5 (3) | SF-06 and SF-12 (developer-facing text shown to end users), SF-15 (the error colour means "changed"), SF-31 (an error page with no way back), SF-37 (errors as plain monospace text). |
| 10 | Help and documentation | none (0) | **No finding in the Stage 1 scope.** The pages read have no help link, tour or documentation page; the only pointers are a support e-mail address on the register and Google-callback pages (S-06, S-07; `src/pages/Register.tsx:59-62`, `src/pages/GoogleRedirectHandler.tsx:55-64`) and the helper text under some fields. A help surface would add a region or a route (Stage 2); the wording of the existing helper texts is Phase 5. |

### Law cross-check

A short check of the perception and cognition laws against what the entries record. Each line names what Stage 1 can change (size, spacing, emphasis, copy inside existing containers) and what it cannot (moving controls, regrouping navigation).

- **Fitts (target size and distance).** The Kiwi Chat launcher is a 56 px circle in a screen corner (SR-6 block 1), an easy target, but its resting place covers content (TD-18, Stage 2). Send is an icon-only `size="large"` button on its own row under the textarea, away from where the pointer is while typing (`src/components/Chat/ChatField.tsx:175-182`; Enter also sends). On the Scratchpad, Run and Upload are default-size icon buttons in the editor's corner (`src/components/CodeInterpreter/IDE.tsx:497-512`) while three equally weighted filled buttons sit below the output (TD-15), so the likely next action has no larger target than the others. Sizes and emphasis are Phase 3; moving the controls is Stage 2.
- **Hick (number of choices).** An admin sees 11 flat sidebar entries (10 built-in and one admin app in the tour) and nine more inside a class (SR-3, Default). Grouping them would shorten the choice, but adding or removing navigation items is outside Stage 1; the card sort and tree test named under IA are the Stage 2 input.
- **Miller and Cowan (working memory, about four chunks).** The same 11-entry sidebar has no grouping, so it asks for more than four chunks at once. The experiment form (A-20) is chunked better: four section cards, each with a heading, and one sticky action card. Spacing and headings inside the existing containers (Phase 3) can make chunks easier to see; regrouping is Stage 2.
- **Recognition over recall.** Icon-only controls with no visible label: the chat header's four mode buttons and close (tooltips and aria-labels only, SR-6 block 3), the composer's Send, the Scratchpad's Run and Upload (S-03 block 8), the Materials icon rail (SF-34) and the Experiment Assistant's close and send buttons (SF-18). The slash commands are findable only through the composer placeholder. Tooltips and accessible names are copy (Phase 5, inside Stage 1); visible labels would change a region (Stage 2).

Counts: 46 entries = 1 root layout + 3 layout routes + 2 `<Navigate>` redirects + 40 leaf routes. The 40 leaf routes include `/logout` (redirect-only, R-3) and `*`; the other 39 map to 34 page entries because four components serve several routes (`ClassEdit` 2, `ExperimentForm` 2, `AdminAppPage` 3, `AnnotateDocument` 2). Redirect rows R-1, R-2 and R-3 are listed with the redirects; R-4 (`/consent` without a consent form) is conditional and is page S-09 when the form is configured.

Appendix A omits two things the code has: the explicit `batch-upload` redirect (a plan edge case) and the effective guard of `/authorize` and `*`, which carry none. Its "all" for `/logout` is signed-in only in code.

### Appendix C · Outgoing email templates (as-is)

Phase 3 maps these literal colours to tokens (DW-3.6) and Phase 5 revises their copy. Source of the three browser-side templates: `src/components/Email/TemplatedEmailForm.tsx`; the backend renders the version recipients actually receive.

**What leaves the browser.** *Custom Email* (A-04, A-17 admin tab): the browser sends a bare HTML string, the Message with every newline replaced by `<br/>`, plus the text (`src/components/Email/EmailComposer.tsx:76-77`). *Templated Email* (A-04): only the template name, its data and the recipients; no HTML (`src/pages/admin/AdminEmail.tsx:224-232`). *Announcement* (A-17): the class id, a data object and an optional subject (`src/pages/admin/ClassEmail.tsx:170-180,219-229`). *Email Selected* (A-16): template names and data (`src/components/Email/EmailSelectedUsersDialog.tsx:134-163`). The HTML in `emailTemplates` is built only for the on-screen preview (`TemplatedEmailForm.tsx:446-453,478-485`).

**Shared stylesheet of the three browser-side templates** (`TemplatedEmailForm.tsx:46-94`): `font-family: Arial, sans-serif`, `line-height: 1.6`; a card up to 600 px wide, radius 8, centred; header 24 px / 700 / centred, padding `30px 24px`; content padding `30px 24px`; footer 12 px, centred. Literal colours: body text `#333333`; page `#f5f5f5`; card `#ffffff`; card shadow `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.1)`; **header background `#5C3B6E`, header text `#ffffff`**; content text `#333333`; footer background `#f8f9fa`, footer text `#666666`, footer top border `1px solid #e9ecef`; links `#5C3B6E`. No colour is purple by name; `#5C3B6E` is the Material-era purple the brief bans.

| Template (subject) | Structure | Extra literal colours | Copy emitted |
|---|---|---|---|
| `classAnnouncement`, "Class Announcement: {{className}}" (`TemplatedEmailForm.tsx:97-140`) | card, header, content, footer; no button | none | header "Class Announcement"; sub-header the class name; optional "From:" and the professor; the message as typed (not escaped, newlines not converted); footer "This email was sent from Kiwi Platform. Please do not reply to this email." |
| `adminNotification`, "Important Notification from Kiwi Platform" (`:142-179`) | card, two-line header, content (an `h3` and a highlighted box), footer | `h3` `#5C3B6E`; message box background `#fff3cd`, border `#ffeaa7`, text `#856404` | header "Kiwi Platform"; sub-header "Important Notification"; the title; the message; footer "This is an automated message from Kiwi Platform Administration. Please do not reply to this email." |
| `welcome`, "Access granted for Kiwi" (`:181-233`) | card, one-line header, paragraphs, one inline link; a `.btn` rule exists and no element uses it | `.btn` `#5C3B6E` on `#ffffff`, hover `#4a2d5a` (unused) | header "Welcome!"; "Dear Students,"; "You have been granted access to Kiwi as per your request. You can now log in from the website." (link on \"website\"); when a support address is configured, \"If you have any issues while exploring Kiwi, please contact us at: {SUPPORT_EMAIL}\"; \"Happy Kiwi-ing!\"; \"Kiwi Support Team\"; the shared footer |

**What recipients receive.** Templated and announcement emails are rendered by the backend from `../kiwi/src/email/email.templates.ts`. The stylesheet, `classAnnouncement` and `adminNotification` are identical to the browser copy. The backend `welcome` differs: header "Welcome to {institutionName or Kiwi}!" instead of "Welcome!", an extra paragraph of the same title, a default link `http://localhost:3001/` instead of the site origin, an optional paragraph with a link on "guide" ("Please follow this guide to learn about how to best use Kiwi to support your self‑learning experience with AI."), and a `.btn:hover` of `#4a3053`. A fourth template exists only in the backend, `classEnrollment` ("You've been added to {{className}} on Kiwi"), and no `kiwi-ui` file builds it (`../kiwi/src/email/email.templates.ts:72-138,224-298`). Whether the backend wraps the Custom Email HTML in a layout is unverified. Default copy typed into the Email Selected dialog: "You have been added to {class}. … You can now access all class materials and discussions. … If you have any questions, please contact your instructor." (`EmailSelectedUsersDialog.tsx:111-121`).
